# PRODUCTION AUDIT — KoruBeni (com.poyrazoncel.korubeni)

> Audit of every individual checkbox in [`docs/MASTER_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`](docs/MASTER_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md).

> Generated 2026-08-12 against git HEAD `fe83771` (branch `feat/tutundurma-ucretsiz-prova`).


**Scope note.** KoruBeni is an Android-only, offline-first Flutter personal-safety app with **no developer backend, no accounts, no analytics/telemetry, and no AI**. A large share of this checklist targets server/web/AI products; those requirements are marked `N/A` with a per-item justification, never for difficulty. Where a requirement genuinely applies but could not be verified with available evidence it is marked `UNVERIFIED`, and where an external dependency blocks it, `BLOCKED`.


## Verification method codes

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `RUN` | Live app interaction — Android emulator `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` (API 36), `app-play-debug`, full first-run walkthrough with screenshots, 2026-08-12 |
| `TEST` | Automated test evidence (`flutter test`, Kotlin unit, Android instrumentation) |
| `CMD` | Command output captured during this audit |
| `SRC` | Concrete source/config implementation where runtime verification does not apply |
| `DOC` | Version-controlled repository document |
| `NONE` | No verification possible with available evidence |

## Baseline commands run

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `flutter analyze --no-fatal-infos` | **No issues found!** (2.9s) |
| `flutter test --no-pub` | **1009 passed / 1 failed** — the failure is `release_change_classification_test.dart` reporting `UNCLASSIFIED_PATH docs/MASTER_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`, i.e. the release gate correctly refusing an unclassified file. Not an app defect. |
| `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` | **CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS** — all 5 critical safety files ≥90% |
| `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` (clean worktree) | **OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS** — 197 pub + 203 maven queries, `findingCount: 0` |
| `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` (clean worktree) | **RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS** — 706 text + 46 binary files, 0 findings |
| `dart format --output=none --set-exit-if-changed lib/ test/` | **63 of 388 files would change** — no format gate in CI (deliberate, see MP-27-003) |
| `flutter build apk --debug --flavor play --target-platform android-arm64` | **Built successfully** (`app-play-debug.apk`) |
| App runtime | **Launched and driven on emulator** — consent gate → onboarding → contact validation → PIN setup → permission rationale/denial → home → settings |

## Result summary

> **Single source of truth.** This table is REGENERATED from the requirement rows below on every
> update. An earlier revision shipped five stacked, contradictory summary tables (INDEPENDENT_REVIEW.md
> IR-05); they were deleted rather than reconciled by hand so the numbers cannot drift again.

| Metric | Count |
|---|---|
| **TOTAL INDIVIDUAL REQUIREMENTS** | **1714** |
| PASS | 485 |
| FAIL | 21 |
| PARTIAL | 140 |
| BLOCKED | 28 |
| N/A | 773 |
| UNVERIFIED | 267 |

### Severity of non-PASS findings

| Severity | Count |
|---|---|
| P0 | 2 |
| P1 | 25 |
| P2 | 228 |
| P3 | 201 |

> **P0 is no longer claimed to be structurally absent.** The previous wording enumerated only
> server/web hazards (auth bypass, tenant leakage, prompt injection) and concluded no catastrophic
> surface exists. That enumeration is correct but incomplete: for THIS product the catastrophic
> event is **the panic button failing to dial**, and `MP-22-001` / `MP-54-029` reach it — a paying
> subscriber offline beyond the 7-day entitlement grace loses SOS. Both are now classified **P0**,
> accepted-with-rationale, pending an explicit product decision. See INDEPENDENT_REVIEW.md IR-04.

## 4. Design System

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/app_theme.dart (233 lines): one ThemeData pair defines colours and component themes. Values are literals inside component themes rather than a named token layer.


**Gap.** There is no design-token layer: no named spacing, radius, elevation, z-index, motion-duration, easing, breakpoint, icon-size or density scale. Widgets consume raw numbers.


**Remediation.** Add lib/core/design_tokens.dart holding the scales and migrate app_theme.dart plus the highest-traffic widgets onto it. This is a maintainability investment, not a user-visible defect.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-04-001` | Design token sistemi var. | Applicable | **FAIL** | P2 | SRC: no token module exists anywhere under lib/. | Design tokens are absent as a concept. | Create lib/core/design_tokens.dart as the single source for all scales. | `SRC` |
| `MP-04-002` | Color token'ları var. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/app_theme.dart: ColorScheme is defined once per theme and consumed via Theme.of(context). | - | - | `SRC` |
| `MP-04-003` | Semantic color token'ları var. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | SRC: Material ColorScheme supplies primary/secondary/error/surface semantics, but product-specific safety semantics (armed, safe, degraded) are literal colours at call sites. | Safety-state colours are not semantic tokens. | Add semantic safety-state colour tokens to the token file. | `SRC` |
| `MP-04-004` | Typography token'ları var. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | SRC lib/core/app_theme.dart themes TextTheme centrally, but several screens still pass literal TextStyle values. | Typography partially bypasses the theme. | Route the remaining literal TextStyles through TextTheme. | `SRC` |
| `MP-04-005` | Spacing scale var. | Applicable | **FAIL** | P2 | SRC lib/core/app_theme.dart contains no such scale; the values appear as literals at call sites. | This scale does not exist as a named system. | Define the scale in lib/core/design_tokens.dart and migrate call sites incrementally. | `SRC` |

## 7. Responsive / Adaptive layout

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/main.dart pins portraitUp/portraitDown and AndroidManifest.xml sets PROPERTY_COMPAT_ALLOW_RESTRICTED_RESIZABILITY=true - Google's documented temporary opt-out from Android 16 large-screen resizability. RUN exercised the 1080x2400 phone form factor only.


**Gap.** Tablet, foldable, landscape and split-screen layouts are explicitly deferred by that manifest opt-out; no adaptive-layout work exists.


**Remediation.** The opt-out expires when the app targets API 37. Start the adaptive-layout workstream now: define breakpoints, verify the four highest-traffic screens on tablet and foldable emulators, and record evidence under docs/qa/.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 8. Bütün component state'leri

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 37


**Section assessment.** RUN exercised default, filled, focused, invalid, valid, disabled and pressed states on the real components (contact form, PIN pad, primary CTAs, locked Pro cards). No exhaustive state matrix or visual-regression suite exists.


**Gap.** There is no systematic component-state matrix and no visual-regression harness, so state coverage is incidental rather than guaranteed.


**Remediation.** Add golden/visual-regression tests for the shared widgets in lib/widgets/ covering the state matrix, or document the matrix as a manual QA sheet under docs/qa/.

## 9. Motion / animasyon

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 28


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/services/reduced_motion_policy.dart centralises the motion on/off decision, and TEST test/screens/dispatch_path_latency_contract_test.dart pins the motion contract on the dispatch path: no transition animation before the countdown, slow 'breathing' pulses instead of blinking, and reduce-motion routing for both the countdown and the panic button.

## 10. UI fiziği

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** The product has no drag-and-drop surface and no custom spring physics; scrolling and gestures use Flutter defaults. The one bespoke gesture is the panic press-and-hold gate (lib/core/utils/panic_hold_gate.dart), which is test-pinned.


**Gap.** Scroll and gesture behaviour is inherited from the framework and was only spot-checked on an emulator; no physical-device feel pass is recorded.


**Remediation.** Fold a scroll/gesture feel check into the existing physical-device QA matrix rather than building instrumentation for framework defaults.

## 12. Accessibility

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 40


**Section assessment.** Targeted accessibility tests exist for the safety-critical surfaces: TEST test/widgets/panic_button_a11y_test.dart, test/screens/countdown_live_region_test.dart, test/screens/timeline_delete_semantics_test.dart. Semantics wrappers appear in 16 files including lib/main.dart. Reduce-motion and 200% text scaling are both honoured and test-pinned.


**Gap.** No screen-reader (TalkBack) pass, no keyboard pass and no automated accessibility-guideline assertions exist. Flutter ships meetsGuideline matchers (androidTapTargetGuideline, textContrastGuideline, labeledTapTargetGuideline) and none are used.


**Remediation.** Add meetsGuideline assertions to the existing widget tests for the primary screens, then run one TalkBack pass on a physical device and record it under docs/qa/.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-12-001` | Bütün app mouse'suz kullanılabiliyor. | Applicable | **UNVERIFIED** | P2 | RUN used touch input only; no hardware keyboard was attached to the emulator and no keyboard traversal test exists. | Keyboard operability is entirely unverified. On an Android touch product this is a lower-likelihood path than TalkBack, but it is also the path an external keyboard or switch-access user depends on. | Attach a hardware keyboard to the emulator, walk the consent -> contact -> PIN -> home flow, and record the result; add focus-order widget tests for the consent and contact forms. | `TEST` |

## 14. Localization / Internationalization

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/main.dart: supportedLocales is [tr_TR] only, startLocale tr_TR, and a non-Turkish persisted locale is actively cleared at startup. TEST test/translations_key_parity_test.dart enforces TR/EN key parity, test/translation_key_usage_test.dart checks usage, and test/main_locale_test.dart + test/main_start_locale_test.dart pin the locale contract.


**Gap.** The first production runtime is deliberately Turkish-only, so the multi-locale requirements below are forward-looking rather than shipped gaps.


**Remediation.** Keep the en-US catalogue as the parity reference it already is; revisit this section when a second locale actually ships.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 19. Error states

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 26


**Section assessment.** SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. The only HTTP surface is the OSM tile client (lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart), which uses package:http RetryClient, enforces a response-size cap (OSM_TILE_RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE), and caches with an explicit policy. RevenueCat handles its own transport.


**Gap.** There is no application-owned API, so per-status-code handling is concentrated in one tile client plus the billing SDK; individual HTTP status codes are not separately handled or tested.


**Remediation.** Add tile-client tests for the status codes that can actually occur against OSM (429, 5xx, malformed body) to test/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client_test.dart, which already exists.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-19-001` | 400. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | SRC lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart: non-success responses fall through to the cached-tile fallback (fallbackLifetime 7 days) rather than being handled per status code. There is no application API where these codes carry distinct meaning. | Status codes are handled as one generic failure class rather than individually. | For a tile CDN this collapse is largely correct; add explicit 429 back-off handling, which is the one code with distinct required behaviour. | `SRC` |
| `MP-19-002` | 401. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | SRC lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart: non-success responses fall through to the cached-tile fallback (fallbackLifetime 7 days) rather than being handled per status code. There is no application API where these codes carry distinct meaning. | Status codes are handled as one generic failure class rather than individually. | For a tile CDN this collapse is largely correct; add explicit 429 back-off handling, which is the one code with distinct required behaviour. | `SRC` |
| `MP-19-003` | 403. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | SRC lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart: non-success responses fall through to the cached-tile fallback (fallbackLifetime 7 days) rather than being handled per status code. ## 20. Offline / kötü bağlantı

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 11


**Section assessment.** Offline-first is the product's core constraint (CLAUDE.md rule 1): the emergency call path has zero network dependency. RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). The offline banner appeared and cleared correctly during the walkthrough.

## 21. Authentication

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 26


**Section assessment.** There is no server identity system. The only authentication is a local 4-digit PIN: SRC lib/core/security/pin_hasher.dart (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 100k iterations, 16-byte salt, constant-time compare) with SRC lib/core/services/pin_lockout_service.dart (exponential backoff from the 5th failure, capped at 15 minutes). Biometric unlock is strictly forbidden by CLAUDE.md rule 2 under a duress threat model, and RUN confirmed no biometric affordance is offered.


**Gap.** Account-oriented requirements below do not exist as attack surface because the product has no accounts, no email, no password and no server session.


**Remediation.** Keep it that way - adding an account tier would create the credential-theft and duress-unlock surface this design deliberately avoids.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

There is no identity to recover or link. SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. | - | Deliberately not applicable. The duress model in .claude/rules/common/security.md makes a recoverable, server-backed identity an anti-goal for this product. | `SRC` |

## 22. Authorization

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 14


**Section assessment.** The only authorization decision in the product is the Free/Pro entitlement gate. It is enforced in three independent layers: SRC lib/core/services/subscription_gate.dart (with bounded timeouts), lib/widgets/panic_button.dart, lib/screens/countdown_screen.dart, and the native Kotlin path. Entitlement itself is issued by RevenueCat/Google Play, which is a real server-side authority outside this codebase. RUN confirmed the gate fails closed when entitlement cannot be resolved.


**Gap.** There is no application server, so 'server-side authorization' means 'the store's entitlement service'. A determined local attacker with a rooted device can tamper with the cached entitlement anchor - the app mitigates with safe_device root detection but cannot fully prevent it.


**Remediation.** Accept the residual local-tamper risk: it is inherent to an offline-capable paid feature and is documented in docs/audit/panik-entitlement-agi-bagimliligi-2026-07-31.md. Do not weaken the offline grace window to chase it - offline Pro access is a safety requirement.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Boundary pinned at exactly 7 days, plus future-anchor and fresh-install-offline cases. Live: the SOS control renders greyed with a PRO badge and 'Kilitli - Provasini ucretsiz calistir'. Mitigation shipped: SubscriptionAccessState now exposes remainingOfflineGrace(), isOfflineGraceExpiring() and hasLostAccessToOfflineGraceExpiry() so the product can warn BEFORE the cliff; previously offlineGracePeriod was referenced only inside its own file. | A PAYING subscriber offline beyond the 7-day grace loses the panic button. For this product the catastrophic class is 'the panic button does not dial', so this is P0-class, not P1. The advance-warning API exists and is test-covered but is NOT yet surfaced in the UI. | PRODUCT DECISION REQUIRED (not invented here): whether panic/SOS should be entitlement-gated at all. Non-controversial work already done: reproduction, regression coverage, and the pre-expiry warning API. Remaining non-controversial work: surface the warning on the home readiness card. | `TEST` |

## 26. Notifications

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/services/notification_service.dart uses flutter_local_notifications (pinned >=19.1.0 to skip a v19.0.x release-mode bug). All notifications are LOCAL - there is no push service, no FCM, and no token. RUN exercised the permission rationale and the denial path; the notification presentation itself was not observed because permission was denied.


**Gap.** Notification rendering, deep-link behaviour and content were not verified at runtime in this session.


**Remediation.** Re-run the walkthrough granting POST_NOTIFICATIONS and capture the check-in and countdown notifications; TEST android/app/src/test/.../NativeNotificationTextTest.kt and FullScreenIntentNotificationTest.kt already pin the native text and full-screen-intent policy.

Cause: PanicButton measures the hold with a REAL monotonic Stopwatch (correct -- a wall-clock change must not complete an armed hold), while tester.pump advances only the FakeAsync clock, so the assertion actually depended on machine load. ## 28. Backend/API

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 24


**Section assessment.** SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. The app exposes no endpoint and owns no server. Its only outbound calls are OSM tile GETs and the RevenueCat SDK, both third-party services governed by their own contracts.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes a developer backend an explicit anti-goal: the emergency call path must work with zero network. Introducing an API would create the availability dependency this product exists to avoid.

## 29. Database

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 31


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/services/local_database_service.dart: a local sqflite database with four tables - contacts (id PK AUTOINCREMENT, name NOT NULL, phone NOT NULL UNIQUE, is_primary NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at NOT NULL), activity_events (id TEXT PK), crash_logs (id PK AUTOINCREMENT), app_settings (key TEXT PK). Note the single-source-of-truth rule in .claude/rules/common/security.md: emergency contacts live in flutter_secure_storage under emergency_contacts_v1, and the DB contacts table is deliberately kept EMPTY.


**Gap.** The onUpgrade path recreates rather than migrates (_onUpgrade calls _onCreate), and there is no versioned migration history. For a local cache whose authoritative store is elsewhere this is largely safe, but it is not a migration system.


**Remediation.** Document the recreate-on-upgrade behaviour as intentional, and add a test asserting that no user-authoritative data lives in the sqflite tables so a future contributor cannot make the recreate destructive.

## 30. Cache

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 10


**Section assessment.** The only cache is the OSM map-tile cache: SRC lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart, with an explicit cache policy, a 7-day fallback lifetime, a response-size cap and address validation. TEST test/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client_test.dart covers it.

## 31. File uploads

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 20


**Section assessment.** SRC: the app uploads nothing - there is no server to upload to. image_picker is present for the optional fake-call caller photo, which is stored locally and never transmitted; share_plus exports a local file through the system share sheet under user control.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The relevant local-file risks that do apply are covered elsewhere: sensitive temp-file purging (section 29 item 23) and export-file hygiene (lib/core/services/sensitive_temp_file_service.dart).

## 32. Klasik application security

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 49


**Section assessment.** The attack surface is a single local Android app with no server. Controls verified this session: CMD `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` on a clean worktree -> RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS, text=706 binary=46 findings=0. CMD `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` on a clean worktree -> OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS, pub_queries=197, maven_queries=203, status=PASS, findingCount=0. SRC android/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml enforces cleartextTrafficPermitted=false; AndroidManifest sets allowBackup=false and excludes every domain from cloud-backup/device-transfer; safe_device provides root/jailbreak detection; DOC docs/release/masvs_assessment.md carries a MASVS assessment gated by TEST test/masvs_assessment_gate_test.dart.

## 34. Privacy

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 28


**Section assessment.** Privacy is a first-class product constraint. SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. RUN verified granular, individually revocable KVKK consents with plain-language purpose and retention statements per data category, plus a separate third-party consent dialog when adding another person's number. DOC docs/kvkk_veri_isleme_envanteri.md is the data inventory; store/DATA_SAFETY_FORM.md and store/google_play_data_safety_answers.md carry the Play declarations.

## 40. Web performance

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 28


**Section assessment.** SRC: Android-only Flutter app. No ios/ or web/ directory exists; flutter_launcher_icons and flutter_native_splash both set ios:false/web:false. Google Play is the only distribution channel. Core Web Vitals (LCP/INP/CLS), bundle size, code splitting, hydration, CDN and HTTP caching are browser concepts with no analogue in a Play-distributed Flutter binary.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The equivalent mobile performance requirements are covered in section 41.

## 41. Native/mobile performance

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** The performance budget that matters is defined in .claude/rules/common/performance.md as 'panic press -> dialling, with screen off, Doze active, and OEM battery savers hostile'. TEST test/screens/dispatch_path_latency_contract_test.dart pins the structural side of that budget (no fixed delay before arming, no transition animation on the dispatch path, haptics not awaited). A nightly emulator matrix (.github/workflows/nightly-android.yml, API 29-36) exercises connected safety tests.


**Gap.** No timing, memory, CPU, GPU or battery measurement from a physical device is recorded for the current build. The project's own rules state that the Doze race is device-only and must never be faked in a unit test.


**Remediation.** Execute the existing store/REAL_DEVICE_QA_MATRIX.md on physical hardware and record results under docs/audit/, as the repository's own convention requires. Emulator results cannot substitute for the Doze and OEM-kill scenarios.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-41-001` | Cold startup. | Applicable | **UNVERIFIED** | P2 | SRC lib/main.dart keeps startup lean (critical bootstrap awaited, best-effort services run concurrently and cannot change the bootstrap decision); TEST test/main_startup_init_order_test.dart pins that order. | Startup time is architecturally controlled but unmeasured against Android's 2-second feedback threshold. | Measure cold/warm/resume start on a physical device and record it. | `NONE` |

## 42. Scalability

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 26


**Section assessment.** SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. There is no shared service to load, saturate or autoscale. Each installation is an independent single-user device; adding a millionth user adds no load to any component the project operates.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The only shared dependencies are OpenStreetMap tiles and RevenueCat, both third-party services with their own capacity. The tile client's persistent cache and size cap are the relevant courtesy controls and they exist (section 30).

## 43. Reliability

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 31


**Section assessment.** The reliability question for this product is inverted: because the emergency path has no developer backend (CLAUDE.md rule 1), no service outage can prevent a panic call. The failure domains that DO exist are device-local - process death, reboot, Doze, revoked permissions - and each has an explicit native control plus tests: android/.../DeviceProtectedEmergencySessionStore.kt, BootCompletedReceiver, CountdownAlarmReceiver ('C4 safety net: fires if Dart timer freezes under Doze'), ExactAlarmPermissionReceiver, ClockChangeReceiver.

## 44. Observability

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 39


**Section assessment.** Observability is deliberately local-only. SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. SRC lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart records allowlisted structured LocalErrorCode values on-device; lib/core/services/crash_log_service.dart writes to the local crash_logs table; android/.../NativeSafetyEventRing.kt keeps a native safety event ring (TEST NativeSafetyEventRingTest.kt); lib/core/services/health_check_service.dart and startup_diagnostics_service.dart provide local self-checks.


**Gap.** There is no remote telemetry of any kind, so the operator cannot observe production error rates, crash rates, latency or adoption. Every requirement in this section that presumes a central collector is unmet by design.


**Remediation.** This is a deliberate KVKK trade-off, not an oversight - but it must be accepted explicitly, because it means the only production signal available is Play Console vitals (crash/ANR rates, which Google provides without an SDK) plus user reports. Document that dependency, and make Play Console vitals monitoring an explicit named operational duty in the launch runbook.

## 45. SLO / alerting

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** No SLI, SLO, error budget, alert, synthetic monitor, uptime check or status page exists for this product.


**Gap.** There is no service to measure availability of, so classic SLOs do not transfer - but the product does have one measurable objective it currently does not track: the panic-press-to-dial budget defined in .claude/rules/common/performance.md, and the Play Console crash/ANR rates that Google publishes without an SDK.


**Remediation.** Define two concrete objectives that are actually observable for this architecture: (1) Play Console crash-free session rate and ANR rate thresholds, with a named owner who checks them on a stated cadence after each release; (2) the panic-press-to-dial latency budget, verified per release on physical hardware rather than continuously monitored. Write both into docs/release/ alongside the existing safety case.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| `MP-45-002` | SLO tanımlı. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC docs/release/observability_and_slo.md (added 2026-08-12). SLO-1 and SLO-2 defined with explicit numbers. | - | - | `DOC` |

## 46. Automated testing

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 37


**Section assessment.** CMD `flutter test --no-pub` -> 1009 passed / 1 failed; the one failure is the release-change-classification gate flagging the untracked checklist doc, not app code. CMD `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` -> CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS; all 5 critical safety files >=90% (emergency_session_contract 99.18%, emergency_platform_service 95.04%, pin_verification_service 94.87%, contact_service 93.08%, check_in_service 90.70%). Plus 18 Kotlin unit-test files and 3 instrumentation test files under android/app/src/, a safety mutation-testing gate (scripts/run_safety_mutations.py, 'Kill mandatory safety mutants' in CI), and a nightly connected-safety emulator matrix across API 29-36.

Cause: PanicButton measures the hold with a REAL monotonic Stopwatch (correct -- a wall-clock change must not complete an armed hold), while tester.pump advances only the FakeAsync clock, so the assertion actually depended on machine load. Fixed with a production-inert test seam (PanicButton.holdClockOverride) plus a controllable stopwatch; verified green on 3 consecutive full-suite runs (1042 tests). | - | - | `TEST` |

## 48. CI pipeline

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** DOC .github/workflows/ci.yml runs on every push to main and every pull request: pub get -> analyze -> test with a critical-coverage gate -> OSV dependency audit -> secret scan (--require-clean) -> Android host unit tests -> safety mutation kill. DOC .github/workflows/release.yml is tag-triggered and verifies a signed annotated tag, main ancestry and checkout/tag match before building. TEST test/release_hardening_contract_test.dart pins action immutability and least-privilege permissions.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-48-001` | Build fail → merge yok. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC .github/workflows/ci.yml: each step runs without `continue-on-error`, so a build failure, test failure, analyzer error or failed security gate fails the job and blocks merge (subject to branch protection, item 6). | - | - | `DOC` |
| `MP-48-002` | Tests fail → merge yok. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC .github/workflows/ci.yml fails the job on any test failure, and the suite is now free of the load-dependent flake that could have masked a real regression. Flaky test found and root-caused 2026-08-12: test/widgets/panic_button_behavior_test.dart passed in isolation but failed ~50% of full-suite runs. Cause: PanicButton measures the hold with a REAL monotonic Stopwatch (correct -- a wall-clock change must not complete an armed hold), while tester.pump advances only the FakeAsync clock, so the assertion actually depended on machine load. Fixed with a production-inert test seam (PanicButton.holdClockOverride) plus a controllable stopwatch; verified green on 3 consecutive full-suite runs (1042 tests). | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-48-003` | Type error → merge yok. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC .github/workflows/ci.yml: each step runs without `continue-on-error`, so a build failure, test failure, analyzer error or failed security gate fails the job and blocks merge (subject to branch protection, item 6). | - | - | `DOC` |

## 50. Deployment

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 20


**Section assessment.** DOC .github/workflows/release.yml: tag-triggered, verifies a signed annotated tag, verifies main ancestry, verifies checkout/tag match, freezes clean source provenance, then builds a signed AAB with build attestation. SRC scripts/verify_release.sh runs the full local gate (analyze -> test -> signed AAB -> Android lint -> Kotlin unit -> 16KB alignment). DOC store/PRODUCTION_ROLLOUT_RUNBOOK.md covers the Play-side rollout.

## 51. Database deployment

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 10


**Section assessment.** SRC: all persistence is on-device - sqflite (local_database_service.dart), flutter_secure_storage (emergency_contacts_v1, user_pin), SharedPreferences (kvkk_consent_log_v2, offline queue). No shared datastore and no tenancy concept exist. There is no operator-run database and therefore no database deployment. The on-device SQLite schema recreates on version upgrade and deliberately holds no authoritative data (contacts live in flutter_secure_storage; see section 29).


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The one genuine local-schema risk - that the recreate-on-upgrade path could someday become destructive - is tracked as the section 29 remediation.

## 52. Backup

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** Backup is deliberately DISABLED and that is the correct security posture here: SRC AndroidManifest.xml sets allowBackup=false and fullBackupContent=false, and android/app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml excludes every domain (root, file, database, sharedpref, external and their device_ counterparts) from both cloud-backup and device-transfer. Under a duress threat model, a cloud copy of a victim's emergency contacts and safety timeline is a liability, not an asset.


**Gap.** There is consequently no user-data recovery path: a lost or wiped device loses the local safety configuration permanently.


**Remediation.** Keep backups disabled. Ensure the onboarding copy sets that expectation - it already says the PIN cannot be reset because there is no server, which is the same message.

CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |

## 53. Disaster Recovery

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 14


**Section assessment.** The only disaster domains that exist are the source repository, the signing key and the Play Console account - not user data (which lives solely on user devices) and not infrastructure (which does not exist).


**Gap.** The catastrophic scenario for this project is loss of the release keystore: DOC per the project's own notes, korubeni_keystore_release.jks is the only keystore with a known password. Losing it means losing the ability to update the app for existing users unless Play App Signing holds the app key.


**Remediation.** Confirm and document whether Play App Signing is enrolled (which makes the upload key replaceable and neutralises this risk entirely). If it is not, enrol it. Record the outcome in docs/release/ - this is the single highest-value DR action available to this project.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

No shared datastore and no tenancy concept exist. There is no central database to lose, corrupt or mass-delete - each device holds only its own owner's data. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |
| `MP-53-002` | Region failure. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. There is no region, no failover target and no DNS the project operates. Google Play's own availability is Google's concern. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |

## 56. Analytics

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 20


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. The product ships no analytics SDK, no event pipeline and no funnel instrumentation, deliberately: .claude/rules/common/security.md states 'No developer backend, no analytics, no ad SDKs', and the in-app KVKK text tells users the app collects no advertising or attribution identifier.


**Gap.** The operator is consequently blind to activation, conversion, retention, churn and funnel behaviour. That is a real commercial cost, accepted in exchange for a privacy claim the product makes to users and to Play.


**Remediation.** Do not add an analytics SDK - it would contradict a published KVKK commitment and the Data Safety declaration. Use the aggregate reporting Google Play already provides (installs, uninstalls, subscription retention, crash-free rate), which requires no SDK and no new disclosure, and name an owner for it in the launch runbook.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-56-001` | Activation. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. ## 57. Experiment / A/B testing

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 10


**Section assessment.** SRC: there is no experimentation framework, no variant assignment and no exposure logging. This follows from having neither a backend nor analytics - a variant could be assigned locally but its effect could never be measured.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. If pricing experiments are ever wanted, Google Play's own store-listing experiments run outside the app and require no SDK.

## 58. SEO — public web ürünüyse

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** SRC: Android-only Flutter app. No ios/ or web/ directory exists; flutter_launcher_icons and flutter_native_splash both set ios:false/web:false. Google Play is the only distribution channel. The only web surface is the GitHub Pages site under .gh-pages-publish/, which exists solely to host the privacy policy and terms at a stable URL for Play Console. It is not a marketing site and is not intended to rank.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The equivalent discipline for this product is App Store Optimisation of the Play listing, which is covered by DOC store/google_play_listing.md, store/play_store_listing_tr.md and TEST test/store_release_docs_test.dart.

## 59. Mobile platform-specific

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 30


**Section assessment.** Android is the only target. Verified: RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). plus the nightly connected-safety matrix across API 29-36 (.github/workflows/nightly-android.yml) and DOC store/ containing the full Play submission pack (data safety, content rating, permissions declaration, listing copy, reviewer access notes).


**Gap.** The 17 iOS requirements do not apply. Several Android requirements are deferred by the deliberate large-screen opt-out, and physical-device evidence for the current build is missing.


**Remediation.** Execute the real-device QA matrix, and treat the large-screen items as the API 37 workstream (section 7).

Passing cases: typedSchedulerAlwaysAcceptsAnInexactBackupOnSupportedDevice, tokenlessLegacyBroadcastCannotDispatch, armIsNeverRefusedForTheOsAlone, theRuntimeSupportsEveryOsThisApkCanBeInstalledOn. | This is emulator evidence. The project's own rules state the Doze race and OEM kill lists cannot be proven off physical hardware. | Execute store/REAL_DEVICE_QA_MATRIX.md on physical devices and record in docs/audit/. Emulator instrumentation now covers the regression surface continuously. | `CMD` |

## 60. UGC / sosyal özellik varsa

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** SRC: the product has no user-generated content, no social feature, no sharing between users, no profiles and no server to host content on. The only user-entered data (an emergency contact's name and number) is private to the device and never transmitted or displayed to anyone else.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The one adjacent obligation - that adding another person's phone number processes their personal data - is handled by the third-party consent dialog verified in RUN (section 34).

## 61. Email

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** SRC: the app sends no email. There is no mail provider, no transactional mail, no server to send from, and TEST test/screens/edit_profile_screen_test.dart proves the email field was deliberately removed from the profile - the product does not even store an address.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The support contact (korubeni.destek@gmail.com) is a published address the user writes to, not a channel the app sends on.

## 62. Legal / compliance

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** This is the most thoroughly evidenced area of the project. SRC lib/constants/legal_texts.dart is the single source for versions and dates, mirrored to store/*.html by scripts/bump_legal.sh. Gates: TEST test/legal_no_first_responder_claims_test.dart, test/public_release_overclaim_copy_test.dart, test/legal_data_export_claims_test.dart, test/store_release_docs_test.dart, test/third_party_notices_test.dart, test/sbom_license_policy_gate_test.dart, test/license_review_queue_test.dart. RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). confirmed the consent gate renders versioned Terms (3.1.0) and KVKK (3.4.0) text with a data-controller identity and contact address.

## 63. Infrastructure / cloud

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 25


**Section assessment.** The project operates no cloud infrastructure. The only 'infrastructure' is GitHub (source, CI, Pages) and the Google Play Console. GitHub-side controls are strong and verifiable: every action pinned to a commit SHA, least-privilege workflow permissions, a production environment for secrets, signed-tag release gating and build attestations (TEST test/release_hardening_contract_test.dart).


**Gap.** Play Console and GitHub account-level settings (MFA, access control, branch protection) live outside the repository and cannot be verified from it.


**Remediation.** Do a one-off account-hygiene pass - confirm MFA on the Google and GitHub accounts, confirm branch protection on main, confirm Play App Signing enrolment - and record the date in docs/release/. That is the whole of this section for this architecture.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-63-001` | Infrastructure as Code. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC .github/workflows/ are the entire infrastructure definition and are version controlled; SRC android/app/build.gradle.kts, config/*.json and scripts/ are likewise in-repo and gated by TEST test/release_change_classification_test.dart, which fails closed on any unclassified path. | - | - | `DOC` |
| `MP-63-002` | Version controlled. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC .github/workflows/ are the entire infrastructure definition and are version controlled; SRC android/app/build.gradle.kts, config/*.json and scripts/ are likewise in-repo and gated by TEST test/release_change_classification_test.dart, which fails closed on any unclassified path. | - | - | `DOC` |
| `MP-63-003` | Network segmentation. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. There is no network, load balancer, DNS zone, CDN, WAF, autoscaling group, quota, capacity plan or taggable cloud resource operated by this project. | - | Not applicable. The app is distributed as a binary through Google Play; Google operates the only serving infrastructure. | `SRC` |

## 64. Cost / FinOps

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** Marginal infrastructure cost per user is effectively zero: no servers, no database, no storage, no egress, no logging pipeline and no AI. The only variable third-party costs are the RevenueCat plan tier and Google Play's revenue share; the only externality is OpenStreetMap tile traffic, which is donated infrastructure the project should not abuse.


**Gap.** No cost model or budget alert exists, though the exposure is small and bounded.


**Remediation.** Two concrete actions: confirm the RevenueCat plan tier covers projected subscribers, and estimate OSM tile requests per active user against OSM's bulk-use policy. Skip the rest of this section - it presumes cloud spend this project does not have.

SRC: no AI/LLM dependency exists. pubspec.yaml declares none, and grep for openai/anthropic/langchain/embedding/vector/llm across lib/ and android/ returns nothing. ## 65. Support

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 17


**Section assessment.** RUN confirmed Settings exposes 'Yardim & Destek (SSS ve iletisim)', the privacy policy (local and web), and app version information. The published contact address korubeni.destek@gmail.com appears in the in-app KVKK text and in the store material.


**Gap.** Support is a single email address with no ticketing, severity model or response-time commitment - and because there is no telemetry, the operator cannot diagnose a user's problem without the user exporting their own local log.


**Remediation.** Write a one-page support process: how a user exports their local data/log, what to ask for, and a stated response expectation. The export mechanism already exists (lib/core/services/user_data_export_service.dart); the missing piece is telling users and support to use it.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 66. Internal admin panel

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 16


**Section assessment.** SRC: no admin panel, no internal tool and no back office exists - there is no server for one to talk to and no user data reachable off-device. The nearest analogue, the in-app Test Mode, is a user-facing rehearsal feature that dials nothing and is deliberately free.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The absence of an admin surface removes the entire class of risk this section covers, which is the correct outcome for a duress-model safety product.

## 67. Incident response

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 16


**Section assessment.** DOC docs/veri_ihlali_bildirimi_proseduru.md defines the KVKK data-breach notification procedure, and DOC store/PRODUCTION_ROLLOUT_RUNBOOK.md covers halting a staged rollout.


**Gap.** There is no general incident runbook. For a solo-maintainer offline app most of this section's roles collapse into one person, but the mechanical questions - how to halt a rollout, how to roll forward, how to rotate credentials - should still be written down before they are needed at 3am.


**Remediation.** Write one short incident document answering only the questions that apply here: who notices (Play Console vitals), how to halt a rollout, how to roll forward (not back - Play forbids versionCode regression), how to rotate the RevenueCat key and GitHub secrets, and when the existing breach procedure triggers. Skip incident-commander role assignment and status pages.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 69. Browser/device matrix

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 17


**Section assessment.** SRC: Android-only Flutter app. No ios/ or web/ directory exists; flutter_launcher_icons and flutter_native_splash both set ios:false/web:false. Google Play is the only distribution channel. The Android equivalent is well covered in CI: .github/workflows/nightly-android.yml runs connected safety instrumentation nightly across API 29, 30, 31, 32, 33, 34, 35 and 36 on a pixel_6 profile, and android/app/src/androidTest/.../SupportedOsBoundaryInstrumentationTest.kt pins the supported OS envelope.


**Gap.** Physical-device coverage for the current build is missing, and input modalities other than touch are unverified.


**Remediation.** Execute store/REAL_DEVICE_QA_MATRIX.md on physical hardware; add one keyboard and one TalkBack pass.

## 70. “Kötü kullanıcı” testi

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 25


**Section assessment.** Several adversarial cases are covered by tests rather than by hand: TEST test/widgets/panic_button_behavior_test.dart (rapid press, release before the hold gate), test/services/consent_manager_corrupt_record_test.dart (malformed stored record must not wipe valid consents), android/app/src/test/.../PanicLaunchTest.kt (a third-party app cannot forge a countdown), and the safety mutation suite. RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). exercised invalid input and permission denial.


**Gap.** Hostile-input cases (huge paste, HTML, script, SQL-like strings, unicode garbage, emoji flood) are not exercised anywhere.


**Remediation.** Add one input-robustness test that pushes hostile strings through the contact name and phone fields. The phone field is already constrained by lib/core/utils/emergency_number_validator.dart, so the name field is the real target.

## 71. “Gerçek insan” kalite testi

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** No usability testing with real users is recorded anywhere in the repository. This session's walkthrough is a proxy at best - the operator already knew the product's model.


**Gap.** The checklist asks for five real users given no help. That has not happened, and for a safety product bought on trust it is a material gap: the failure mode is not 'user is confused', it is 'user believes they are protected when they are not'.


**Remediation.** Run an unmoderated first-run test with five people who have never seen the app. Focus on one question above all: after onboarding, can they correctly state what will happen when they press the panic button, and do they know they must rehearse with Test Mode? The product already has the right instinct here (Test Mode is deliberately free precisely so trust does not have to be bought on faith) - this validates whether that lands.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 72. Görsel “premium polish” turu

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 36


**Section assessment.** RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). Nine full-screen captures were reviewed and no obvious visual defect was found beyond the clipped helper text recorded in section 7. However, no systematic screen-by-screen polish pass was performed, and there is no golden-image suite to catch regressions.


**Gap.** Polish is verified by eye on one device at one density, on a subset of screens. Several items in this list (gradient banding, shadow clipping, optical icon alignment) require deliberate close inspection that was not performed.


**Remediation.** Add Flutter golden tests for the highest-traffic screens - they would both establish the current baseline and catch regressions - then do one deliberate polish pass over the captured goldens.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 73. Release öncesi security gate

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** Read as a NO-GO list: PASS below means the blocking condition is absent. Evidence: CMD `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` on a clean worktree -> OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS, pub_queries=197, maven_queries=203, status=PASS, findingCount=0. CMD `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` on a clean worktree -> RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS, text=706 binary=46 findings=0. CMD `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` -> CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS; all 5 critical safety files >=90% (emergency_session_contract 99.18%, emergency_platform_service 95.04%, pin_verification_service 94.87%, contact_service 93.08%, check_in_service 90.70%).


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-73-001` | Bilinen critical vulnerability varsa. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | No known critical vulnerability. CMD `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` on a clean worktree -> OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS, pub_queries=197, maven_queries=203, status=PASS, findingCount=0. CI re-runs this audit on every push and pull request. | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-002` | Auth bypass varsa. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | No auth bypass is possible because there is no server auth to bypass. The local PIN is PBKDF2-hashed with constant-time comparison and rate-limited (lib/core/services/pin_lockout_service.dart); CMD `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` -> CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS; all 5 critical safety files >=90% (emergency_session_contract 99.18%, emergency_platform_service 95.04%, pin_verification_service 94.87%, contact_service 93.08%, check_in_service 90.70%). covers pin_verification_service at 94.87%. | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-003` | Tenant isolation hatası varsa. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: all persistence is on-device - sqflite (local_database_service.dart), flutter_secure_storage (emergency_contacts_v1, user_pin), SharedPreferences (kvkk_consent_log_v2, offline queue). No shared datastore and no tenancy concept exist. There is no tenancy, so no tenant-isolation error can exist. | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-004` | Remote code execution ihtimali varsa. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | No RCE surface: the app executes no shell command, loads no remote code, evaluates nothing dynamically, and has no WebView. The only remote input is PNG map tiles, size-capped and address-validated. | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-005` | Sensitive data leak varsa. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | No sensitive-data leak path exists: SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. TEST test/main_release_logging_contract_test.dart and test/release_hardening_contract_test.dart pin that release logs and PendingIntents carry no PII. | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-006` | Hardcoded production secret varsa. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | CMD `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` on a clean worktree -> RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS, text=706 binary=46 findings=0. Only the RevenueCat PUBLIC goog_ SDK key ships, enforced by shape at build time in gradle, the release workflow and lib/core/config/app_environment.dart (TEST test/release_readiness_policy_test.dart). | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-007` | Backup yoksa kritik sistemlerde. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | Backup is deliberately disabled and there is no operator-held user data to back up or restore (section 52). Source and release artefacts are protected by git, signed tags and CI attestations. | - | - | `CMD` |
| `MP-73-008` | Restore doğrulanmadıysa kritik sistemlerde. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | Backup is deliberately disabled and there is no operator-held user data to back up or restore (section 52). Source and release artefacts are protected by git, signed tags and CI attestations. | - | - | `CMD` |

## 75. Release öncesi engineering gate

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** CMD `flutter analyze --no-fatal-infos` -> "No issues found!". CMD `flutter test --no-pub` -> 1009 passed / 1 failed; the one failure is the release-change-classification gate flagging the untracked checklist doc, not app code. CMD `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` -> CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS; all 5 critical safety files >=90% (emergency_session_contract 99.18%, emergency_platform_service 95.04%, pin_verification_service 94.87%, contact_service 93.08%, check_in_service 90.70%). CMD `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` on a clean worktree -> OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS, pub_queries=197, maven_queries=203, status=PASS, findingCount=0. CMD `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` on a clean worktree -> RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS, text=706 binary=46 findings=0.


**Gap.** The suite is green apart from one gate failing on the untracked audit documents, and the operational half of this gate (monitoring, alerts, dashboards, on-call) does not exist by architectural choice.


**Remediation.** Classify the new audit documents in config/release_change_classification.json so the release gate returns to green, then decide explicitly which operational items this architecture will simply not have.

## 76. Canary release gate

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** Google Play staged rollout is the canary mechanism available to this product, and DOC store/PRODUCTION_ROLLOUT_RUNBOOK.md describes using it. No rollout has been performed for this build, so no canary signal exists yet.


**Gap.** Most signals this section asks for cannot be observed at all without telemetry; the ones that can (crash rate, ANR rate, subscription conversion) come from Play Console after a rollout begins.


**Remediation.** Before the first staged rollout, write down which Play Console metrics will be watched, at what threshold the rollout halts, and who checks them - see section 45. Then the canary gate becomes executable rather than aspirational.

## 77. Full launch gate

_This heading contains no checkboxes in the source checklist (it is a summary table). No requirements to evaluate._


## 78. Launch sonrası ilk dönem

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 25


**Section assessment.** The product has not launched, so no post-launch observation exists. What is observable after launch is constrained by the deliberate no-telemetry design: Play Console vitals (crash-free rate, ANR rate, excessive wakeups), Play subscription reporting, Play reviews, and user email.


**Gap.** Most items in this section presume telemetry the product will never have.


**Remediation.** Write a short post-launch watch list covering only what is actually observable - Play vitals, subscription retention, store reviews, support volume - with a named owner and a cadence. Explicitly mark the rest as unobservable by design so a future maintainer does not assume the data exists somewhere.

## 79. Her production bug'dan sonra

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** The project already practises much of this: docs/audit/ and docs/qa/ contain dated investigation records, several past bugs became permanent gates (the flutter_local_notifications v19.0.x release-mode bug became a pinned lower bound with a comment; overclaiming copy became test/public_release_overclaim_copy_test.dart; the PIN lockout integer overflow became a documented cap with a pure testable function), and the safety mutation suite exists to prove the tests actually catch regressions.


**Gap.** The practice is real but informal - there is no postmortem template and no rule that a fixed bug must ship with the test that would have caught it (though .claude/rules/common/testing.md does require new emergency-path behaviour to ship with tests).


**Remediation.** Adopt this checklist section itself as the postmortem template and put it in docs/. The habit already exists; writing it down makes it survive a maintainer change.

| `MP-79-014` | Architecture değişikliği gerekiyor mu? | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: the project has made architectural changes in response to findings - the typed native safety kernel replaced EmergencyExecutor/CheckInScheduler, and docs/HANDOVER.md is explicitly marked ARCHIVED to prevent the old architecture being treated as current. | - | - | `DOC` |
| `MP-79-015` | Checklist'e yeni madde eklenmeli mi? | Applicable | **PASS** | - | DOC: the .claude/rules/common/*.md files are exactly this - accumulated rules derived from real incidents, each carrying its rationale (why no biometrics, why the consent log is off the keystore, why dart format is not run repo-wide). | - | - | `DOC` |

## 80. Bir özelliğin “DONE” tanımı

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 17


**Section assessment.** DOC .claude/rules/common/development-workflow.md defines the project's own definition of done: plan and approval first, small steps each with green tests, emergency-path behaviour shipping with its tests in the same commit, review by the appropriate reviewer agent, then scripts/verify_release.sh if the change touches code, manifest, gradle, legal or CI.


**Gap.** The existing definition covers design intent, implementation, tests, security and release verification well. It does not cover accessibility, motion, analytics, monitoring or rollback because several of those do not exist as capabilities in this architecture.


**Remediation.** Extend the workflow rule with the dimensions that genuinely apply and are currently missing - accessibility assertions and a golden test for any new screen - and record explicitly that analytics and monitoring are not part of done for this product, so their absence reads as a decision rather than an oversight.
